# The program

This file ranks every item of work across the hub and the apps. A plan serves one item, quotes its acceptance, and at its close writes the item's state and what changed. New work enters by being ranked here, and the next item taken is the highest in rank that no open dependency blocks.

## Thesis

Every app in the family is free, runs on the reader's own device, works offline after its first load, asks for no account, collects no analytics, keeps no user data on a server, and draws only on honest or open data sources. A change that gives one of these up changes what the app is, and is named before it is made. A build that trades one of them away is a separate product under its own name with its own honest claim, and the default build never weakens to make room for it. The hub is the owner's personal link page for the photography accounts and these free web apps, static and self-contained with no build step, and it is the home of the doctrine, the lessons, the palettes and the gates every app shares. The sessions that build the apps work for the owner, whose part is judgement: approving a plan, reading a result, settling a choice only the owner can settle. Each app keeps its own thesis in its own notes, and the wall question below reads that thesis before it reads anything else.

## How an item moves

### The six states

- open: ranked and waiting. No plan carries it.
- planned: a plan that carries it is written and waits for the owner's button.
- running: an approved plan that carries it is in force.
- parked: it waits on a choice or a setting only the owner can make. The entry names what it waits on and what it unblocks, and the session takes the next item that does not depend on it.
- done: its acceptance was observed. The entry says what was observed and which plan's close recorded it.
- ejected: a plan that carried it closed unfinished. The entry keeps why, the finding that moved it and the dependencies the plan moved, the item is re-ranked, and a plan takes it up only after reading that entry.

An item moves from open to planned to running to done. Any of open, planned or running moves to parked when it comes to wait on the owner, and back to open once the owner has settled it. Running moves to ejected when its plan ejects.

### The three ejects

- A step ejects to the plan. It stops, writes what it saw and the files it names, and the plan is rewritten from those files.
- A plan ejects to the program. It closes unfinished, its item goes back to the list as ejected with the finding attached and is re-ranked, the dependencies it moved are marked, and the next item that does not depend on it is taken.
- The program ejects to the owner. This is only for a choice that is the owner's: a goal, a thesis, a taste, or a trade between two items only the owner can rank. It is put once, with the items it blocks named, that item is parked, and the session takes the next item that does not depend on it.

Each eject is a recorded move with its reason, so a later plan cannot pick the item up without reading why a plan stopped.

### The wall question

One question is answered in writing before a step goes, at every stop inside it, and at a plan's close. It has three parts: does this still serve the item's acceptance, does the item still serve the thesis, and did what was just seen move either. It is read upward and is answered from this file and the thesis, not from the plan, because the plan is the ladder and the wall is what the ladder leans on. A no is an eject. A no on the first part ejects the step to the plan, a no on the second ejects the plan to the program, and a move of a goal, a thesis, a taste or a trade between items ejects the program to the owner.

### Length

No count of lines or characters gates a plan or a design, or shortens one. A plan is as long as what it requires, and so is a design.

### The form of an entry

An entry has a key of the form P-1 and a title, then its repository, goal, state and plan, then its acceptance, which is what is observed when it is done, and its dependencies, which are item keys, queue keys, settings only the owner can change, and pins. The goal is the number of one of these six goals, which are the standing goals of the instruction file and the redesign's own goal 1.

- Goal 1: sessions work the owner's way by structure and not by a model's memory.
- Goal 2: the owner's time and credit go to judgement only.
- Goal 3: the work is visible every few minutes without being asked for.
- Goal 4: only a choice the owner alone can make comes back to the owner.
- Goal 5: an approved plan runs to its end, and what is found on the way is handled and recorded for the next plan.
- Goal 6: the owner's rules bind as written, held by gates that leave the main thread no choice.

The plan field names the plan that carries the item, or none. The redesign plan is the plan of 2 November 2026 whose steps are 50 to 62; it wrote this file.

### Rank

The list is the rank. The items the redesign plan builds come first, then the parked settings, then the harness items (the blind areas and the documented mechanisms), then the knowledge layers, then the dependency updates and the sibling pins, then the findings of the Found queue, in the order the audit of 1 November 2026 ranks them where it names them and by key otherwise.

## Items the redesign plan builds

These ten are Design parts 2 to 11 of the redesign plan, in its order. P-1 is part 2, P-2 is part 3, and so on to P-10, which is part 11. Part 1 is this file, and part 12 is the parked settings that follow.

### P-1. The wall check as a written line

Repository: hub. Goal: 1. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 55 wrote the wall line into the draft of the agent rules at version 5 and into the review file, and step 61 landed version 5; no gate reads the wall line (Q-HUB-176 to Q-HUB-227 hold the findings).

Acceptance: a step's report opens, after its verdict word, with one line answering whether the step still serves the plan's goal, whether that goal still serves the owner's asks, and whether what was seen moved either, and the review's hand-back carries its own such line. Observed in the first plan run under the new rules, and by a case that fails a report without the line.

Depends: P-3, because the step shape carries the line. The agent rules change lands in the redesign plan's last step.

### P-2. The three ejects as recorded moves

Repository: hub. Goal: 5. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 55 made EJECT a hand-back word, a state of its own in the pointer, the status and the page, and a manager note that names an item of this file closes the pointer, with the stop guard accepting the declaration, and cases and plants for each.

Acceptance: a step that meets an observation it did not name hands back EJECT with what was seen and the files named, and the pointer, the status and the page show EJECT as a state of its own; a manager note that opens with Eject, names an item of this file and gives a reason closes the pointer of the plan in force; a plan that ejects to the program closes with its item back here as ejected, the finding attached and the item re-ranked; the program ejects to the owner only for a choice that is the owner's. Observed by cases for the first two and by the first close that uses the others.

Depends: P-1, P-3.

### P-3. The step as a list that cannot be done wrong

Repository: hub. Goal: 1. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 55 added the plan check that refuses a step lacking Facts, Do, Observe or Kill, with no latch, and the draft rules; step 61 landed the agent rules at version 5 with the failed-command rule changed.

Acceptance: every step of a plan is a list of Facts (the asks and app facts it depends on, restated), Do (the exact actions), Observe (the observation that means done) and Kill (what ends it with nothing written); the plan check refuses a plan whose step lacks a label, with no latch; a red check ends the step and no write follows it; the agent rules say so and no longer let a failed command run again after a write. Observed in the agent rules at version 5 and in the cases for the plan shape.

Depends: the agent rules change lands only as the last act of the redesign plan, from the draft its step 55 writes.

### P-4. Conversation as the default

Repository: hub. Goal: 4. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 56 added one phase predicate for the dispatcher and the stop guard (conversation, in force, finished), skipped the holds outside a plan, and held the stop word by what it protects, with five cases and 26 plants.

Acceptance: in a session with no approved plan in force and not in plan mode, a message gets an answer from the files and nothing else, and the manager fence, the correction hold, the stop hold, the status gate, the reads gate, the page gate, the recall gate and the judgement gate do not run, while the approved-plan, push, Drive, keep-information and pending-message guards still do; inside a plan a correction holds sends until it is answered and the word stop holds agents, sends and repository writes while passing reads, the status, the plan file and the manager notes; neither holds the plan file's write in plan mode; the stop guard's refusals on a reply's prose run only under an approved plan. Observed by a case for each, failing without its change, and a plant the suite catches.

Depends: none. The reasons of lessons 376, 384, 395, 406, 428, 434 and 456 are each answered when it lands.

### P-5. The doctrine grouped

Repository: hub. Goal: 2. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 57 moved the 42 sections whole into doctrine/never.md, doctrine/plans.md and doctrine/apps.md, made DOCTRINE.md the index, and narrowed the read guard to the never page at a start and the plan page at the entry to plan mode.

Acceptance: the never-rules are on one short page read whole at every start and after a compaction, the plan rules are read whole at the entry to plan mode, and nothing else is due by the read guard; the product, taste, accessibility and release sections are read when an item points at them; the index in the doctrine file lists every section with its file, the section bodies equal the former file's byte for byte, and no section number cited in the lessons, the instruction file, the notes, the review file or a guard's refusal text is missing from the index.

Depends: the reasons of lessons 383, 385 and 398, which the short page keeps.

### P-6. Configuration under management

Repository: hub. Goal: 6. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 53 wrote CONFIG.md, the baseline of hooks, guard files, rules version, pins, Dependabot, permission rules, branch protection and sibling pins, each with the item that may change it.

Acceptance: a baseline file lists every hook, every guard file with its line count and its count of refusal sites by a stated method, the agent rules version, each action pin, the dependency tool's schedule and open updates, the permission rules, the branch protection the connector reports, each sibling's hub pin and sweep caller, and the environment's own hooks the audit names, each with the item of this file that may change it; a change to any of them is an item, a drift found is a finding that becomes one, and an open dependency update is an item. Observed when the baseline exists with every guard file listed and the docs check exits 0.

Depends: none.

### P-7. Several sessions, one hub

Repository: hub. Goal: 1. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 54 made each finding its own file in found/ and pointed both queue readers at the folder; the per-app append and the plan's pin of the hub commit are not built and stay with the next plans.

Acceptance: each finding is its own file in a folder at the hub root, so two sessions never edit one file, and the reader of the answered-findings check takes keys from that folder where it exists; the program is appended per app with a pull before every write and an eject on conflict; a plan pins the hub commit its gates run from and runs to its end on it; a newer hub is taken up at the next plan and never in the middle of one. The redesign plan builds the folder and the reader. The per-app append and the plan's pin of the hub commit are not built by it, and the next plan's first act, the pull of the live clone, begins them.

Depends: none.

### P-8. Gates as the last catch

Repository: hub. Goal: 6. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: step 58 removed the judgement and tier gates and the status gate's refusal, narrowed the review gate to the first send, a send after a failed hand-back or a correction, made the three loosenings, and wrote a reason line for each lesson named.

Acceptance: every deterministic gate that held is kept; the judgement-line gate and the tier gate are removed, the status gate's refusal is removed while the status print and the reminder stay, and the review gate refuses a step only for the plan's first send, the first send after a hand-back that opens EJECT, FAILED or REFUSED, or the first send after a correction, when the newest review does not clear it; the three loosenings of Q-HUB-122, Q-HUB-123 and Q-HUB-124 are made; each gate removed or narrowed is answered against the written reason of the lesson that made it. Observed by the narrowed cases, every remaining plant caught, and a line for each lesson named.

Depends: P-1, because the wall line takes the judgement line's place, and P-10, because the plan's cost is read against printed figures.

### P-9. The knowledge layer as a shape

Repository: hub. Goal: 1. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: the hub's half only; step 55 gave every step the Facts label and the review a reading against the facts; each app's half is its own item below.

Acceptance: an app's instruction file opens with a short head of the facts a session gets wrong from its prior; tests on that app's own fixtures fail when a fact is broken; the facts are restated in every step that touches them; and the review reads the change against the head. The redesign plan builds the hub's half, which is the Facts label of every step and the review's reading against the head. Each app's half is its own item below, built in that app's own session.

Depends: P-3.

### P-10. The measure

Repository: hub. Goal: 2. State: done. Plan: the redesign plan.

Done: the cost instrument was written in step 30 and its totals for the redesign plan were written as the close entry below by step 60.

Acceptance: at each plan's close the cost instrument prints tokens by tier, the owner's messages, go-words, corrections, refusals and pushes, and the plan's close entry is written under this item, so a plan is judged against the plans before it.

Depends: the cost instrument, which the redesign plan's step 30 wrote.

## Parked settings

These wait on the owner. Each names what it unblocks. The exact values are written into this file by the redesign plan's step 60.

### P-11. Branch protection

Repository: the hub and every app, on main and on staging where an app has one. Goal: 6. State: parked. Plan: none.

Acceptance: the connector's branch listing shows the hub's main protected and each app's main and staging protected, each requiring the repository's checks before a merge and refusing force pushes and deletions.

Unblocks: the stage that removes any gate which stands in for protection by reading the transcript; and Jefferson-Photography-Studio's deploy, which does not wait on its gates while main is unprotected (REPOS.md). The hub's six branches were listed unprotected on 2 November 2026.

Exact values: protect main on njefferson/noahjefferson (the hub has no staging; its five other branches are session branches that the sweep removes). Protect main and staging on njefferson/Quietkeep, njefferson/fauxplane, njefferson/3d-printing-pal, njefferson/Intersecting-parallels and njefferson/photo-pointer, which REPOS.md says carry both. Protect main, and staging where REPOS.md names one, on njefferson/MoleBridge, njefferson/Cv-Thalweg, njefferson/solve-ent, njefferson/Jefferson-Photography-Studio and njefferson/Jefferson-Line. Each rule requires the repository's CI checks before a merge, refuses force pushes and refuses deletion. The hub's push goes straight to main by design, so a rule that requires a pull request there would stop the hub's own deploy; for the hub the rule is the force-push and deletion refusals and the required checks, with no required review.

Depends: the owner, in GitHub's settings. Whether this can be set from a tablet was not read.

### P-12. The environment's git stop hook

Repository: the cloud environment. Goal: 2. State: parked. Plan: none.

Acceptance: the environment's git stop hook is narrowed or turned off, and a session with an agent running receives no stop-hook message about uncommitted or unpushed work.

Unblocks: the main thread's forced turns, forty-two between 20:38 California time on 31 October 2026 and 16:35 on 1 November 2026, each re-reading the whole context; Q-HUB-125.

Known: the hook is wired from the environment's launcher settings, outside any repository. Not known: where it is set. None of the twelve documentation pages read on 2 November 2026 names it.

Exact values: the hook is the environment's, not the hub's. The hub's own stop hook is `.claude/hooks/stop-guard.sh`, wired under Stop in the hub's `.claude/settings.json` (line 38), and that is not the one meant here. The audit found 28 of the plan's 31 stop-hook messages came from the environment's command hook, the git check for uncommitted and unpushed work, and 3 from the hub's stop guard. Narrow it so that it does not fire while a background agent of the session is running, or turn it off; the setting is in the cloud environment's own settings page, which the documentation pages read on 2 November 2026 do not name.

Depends: the owner, in the cloud environment's settings.

### P-13. Permission rules per repository

Repository: the hub and every app. Goal: 6. State: parked. Plan: none.

Acceptance: each repository's settings file holds one prefix rule for each command shape of the plan that runs there, never a bare tool, a wildcard alone or a rule for a whole program, and the file parses.

Unblocks: the harness's automatic mode judging commands the plan lists, which has spoiled whole sessions.

Known: a session cannot write these for itself, because the permission classifier refuses an edit of the session's own list (lesson 370); allow rules apply only once the folder is trusted, and a session holding several repositories reads no permission rules from any repository (the record, section 11); the hub's settings hold 20 allow rules, two of which name a sibling repository's path (the step 32 inventory, 2 November 2026).

Exact values: the hub's `permissions.allow` holds 21 entries (Q-HUB-171 corrects the 20 the step 32 report carried). The shapes of the redesign plan's Commands that have no prefix rule there, one rule each, written in the form the file already uses: `Bash(git show:*)`, `Bash(git rev-parse:*)`, `Bash(git commit-tree:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/session-guards.test.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/docs-check.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/privacy-check.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/quote-check.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/third-person-check.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/lessons-check.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/example-check.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/pin-check.mjs:*)`, `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/session-cost.mjs:*)` and `Bash(node /home/user/noahjefferson/session-brief.mjs:*)`. Already held: git status, diff, log, add, commit and ls-remote, git push, node --check, the reply guard's read command and `node /tmp/claude-0/`. Two entries name the sibling Jefferson-Photography-Studio's path and mean nothing to a session on the hub alone (Q-HUB-146), and the bare tool name Artifact breaks this item's own acceptance (Q-HUB-173); both are the owner's to keep or remove. Each sibling's file gets the shapes its own plans list and no more.

Depends: the owner.

### P-14. The preferences file

Repository: the owner's preferences file, outside any repository. Goal: 2. State: parked. Plan: none.

Acceptance: for each line the record shows costing the owner without protecting the owner, one proposed revision is put to the owner, and the owner's decision is applied. The costs the record shows are the doctrine read every session, the go-word hold, and the manager that never reads source.

Unblocks: sessions that cost less to run and ask less of the owner.

Exact values: three lines, each the record's line (the record's section 12, the preferences item) and one proposed revision beside it.
- The record's line: the doctrine is read every session. Proposed revision: the owner's preference reads that only the never page is read at a start and the plan page at the entry to plan mode, the rest when an item points at it, which is what the guards now hold after the redesign plan lands.
- The record's line: the go-word hold. Proposed revision: a message that ends in a go-word is a go and any other message is answered from the files in the turn it arrives, with sends held only inside an approved plan, which is the conversation-as-default structure of P-4; nothing is asked of the owner but the choice the owner alone can make.
- The record's line: the manager that never reads source. Proposed revision: the main thread may read an app's source in conversation, outside a plan, to answer a question from the files; inside an approved plan the manager still sends agents and does no step itself.

Depends: the owner, and the redesign plan's approval and landing.

### P-15. The model of each part of a plan

Repository: the owner's session settings. Goal: 2. State: parked. Plan: none.

Acceptance: the top tier serves a plan's writing and every eject, and the next tier serves management, with a switch made at those two points only.

Unblocks: the plan's Cost section, which names the tier of each step; and P-18, whose hooks are the way a switch can be made to say what it costs.

Known: each model has its own prompt cache, so a switch means the next request reads the whole conversation with no cache hit (the prompt-caching page, read 2 November 2026).

Exact values and documented settings, each with its address (the maker's pages, read on 2 November 2026 by step 51 of the redesign plan; the report's lines are in the session scratchpad):
- The model setting that uses one model in plan mode and another in execution is the `opusplan` alias: in plan mode it uses opus, in execution mode it uses sonnet. Address: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config. It would put the top tier on a plan's writing and the next tier on its execution with no manual switch, and a switch still makes the next request read the whole conversation with no cache hit. Its fit with a manager that sends agents is not measured.
- The subagent model variable is `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, the default model for subagents, teammates and workflow agents not assigned a model another way; `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE` set to 1 forces one model onto them. Address: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars. Set to the sonnet alias it would hold every agent to the tier the plan's rules name, in place of the plan's own wording.
- The settings that remove the commit and pull-request attribution lines are the `attribution` key, set to false to hide all attribution, and `includeGitInstructions`, which controls the built-in instructions for commits and pull requests. Address: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings. The documentation says the owner's instruction file takes precedence over these lines unless the line is set in managed settings; the reminder appended to each session still asks for a trailer and a link, which Q-HUB-156 and Q-HUB-220 record as costing a commit each time.
- The `plansDirectory` key sets where plan files are stored, default the user's plans folder (same address as the settings). The `env` key sets environment variables for a session, and `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP` is one of them (the hooks and env-vars pages), which P-20 uses.

Depends: the owner.

### P-16. The prompt that asks the owner to approve an update of a published page

Repository: the owner's session settings. Goal: 4. State: parked. Plan: none.

Acceptance: an update of an already published page raises no approval prompt in a session where the owner has allowed the page tool, or the owner knows where the prompt is set and has chosen to keep it.

Known: it appeared once, at the first publish of the status page after a resume that restored plan mode, at about 05:43 California time on 1 November 2026; later publishes in that session raised none (audit finding 11). The hub's settings already carried an allow rule for the page tool when the step 32 inventory read them on 2 November 2026.

Not known: whether the prompt is a permission prompt of the harness or a step on the page side; which setting controls it; whether it recurs outside a plan mode the harness restored. None of the twelve documentation pages read on 2 November 2026 names it.

Exact value: the one place found that touches it is line 4 of the hub's `.claude/settings.json`, the allow rule `Artifact`, a bare tool name that P-13's acceptance excludes (Q-HUB-173). Whether removing it, narrowing it or leaving it changes the prompt was not tested; the prompt's own origin stays unknown.

Unblocks: the status page being updated at every progress reply (Doctrine 7i) without a popup.

Depends: the owner; P-17, because the one observed case followed a resume.

### P-268. The list of paragraphs of the instruction file a gate enforces whole

Repository: hub. Goal: 2. State: parked. Plan: none.

Acceptance: the owner has chosen, from the list in the session scratchpad at `progress/claude-md-list.txt`, which paragraphs of the section of `CLAUDE.md` on the rules that get broken anyway are replaced by the one line proposed for each, and the instruction file stands nearer the maker's target of under 200 lines (it stands at about 1477, and the section is about 777 of them, Q-HUB-147).

## The harness

### P-567. The hub's gates stand down for a dated trial of the harness's own parts, with the approved design on disk and the hub's wiring mapped

Repository: hub. Goal: 1. State: done. Plan: The hub's gates stand down for a trial of the harness's own parts: one switch that every hook honours, the approved design written where a session starts from it, and a map of the hub's wiring

Done: The hub's hooks ask one stand-down module first, with its cases and plants, and the design is recorded in plans/native-setup.md; the trial begins when the stand-down file stands.

Acceptance: a session of the hub repository can be freed, for a dated trial, from every gate of the hub but the few rules with no exceptions: every hook the hub wires honours one stand-down file and passes while it is in force, the push guard and the refusal of the choice popup stay, and deleting the file or the passing of its date brings every gate back; the design approved on 11 November stands in a file of the hub that the instruction file points at; a map of the hub's wiring and of what its plans have cost stands in the scratchpad for the work that follows; each change is proved by a case with the file in force, a case without it and a plant; and the commits are on the remote's main.

Depends: none.

### P-309. The batch seed: the hub's open findings printed as one plan, grouped by the file each names

Repository: hub. Goal: 1. State: done. Plan: the batch seed plan of 5 November, steps 89 to 93.

Done: on 5 and 6 November `plan-seed.mjs` gained `--batch`, with `--size` and `--steps`, which reads the program and the found folder, takes every open finding of the session's repository, groups them by the hub file each names, and prints a plan with a take-up step first, one step per group of up to eight findings that fixes and lands its own commit, and a close step, with the Files, the Found answered and the cost line written from the record; the dispatcher prints the batch at the entry to plan mode where the first open entry of the repository is a finding and the single item otherwise. The single print was put right in the same plan: its step numbers continue from the plan file, it prints one Files label, its Commands are at the working copy's path, its close step carries the landing acts, `--item next` passes over the entries of other repositories and says how many, the review step is never a resend, the cost line names the four occasions on which the review gate asks for a review, and the instruction file's core asks line on reviews says what the review gate asks. The first batch print for the hub's queue, 240 findings in 32 group steps, was printed on 6 November.

Acceptance: each finding of a batch is put right as its own text says, each item is done in the program and the commits are on the remote's main; a plan for the hub's findings is a print and a button.

Depends: P-288.

### P-288. The plan seed: the record printed into the plan at the entry to plan mode

Repository: hub. Goal: 1. State: done. Plan: the plan of 5 November, steps 81 to 83.

Done: on 5 November `plan-seed.mjs` was written with `plans/seed-template.md`, which holds the standing text the owner edits. One command prints, in the plan's own shape, the item's own entry from this file, the goals block, the rules version, the Files list with the found folder, the Commands, the rules of a plan, the close step with its three acts, the review step, the open found keys as a Found answered section, the roadmap and shipped-check stubs, the next step number, and the movable reads (the remote's heads, the live clone's head, the working copy's head) marked as things another session can move. The dispatcher prints it once at the entry to plan mode and the fence and the plan guard pass the command. Four cases and fifteen plants cover it. Delivery of the print as added context at a tool call and at a prompt is not yet shown by a live entry (Q-HUB-256, Q-HUB-257).

Acceptance: a plan starts from a print of the record covering the item's own entry, the Files shape, the close step, the review step, the rules version, the found keys and the movable heads, with the print covered by cases and the next plan written from it.

### P-18. The model-switch hooks

Repository: hub. Goal: 2. State: done. Plan: the plan of 4 November, steps 71 and 72.

Done: step 71 made the dispatcher serve both events. The pre-switch hook allows every switch, writes a line into `status/switches.txt` of the session scratchpad and shows the tokens re-sent, whether the cache is warm and the estimated cost in its system message; it never asks, denies or blocks, since an ask is treated as a refusal on every surface but the terminal's own command. The post-switch hook writes the same line and hands the model the tier line as added context. The dispatcher's install wires both events, and `CONFIG.md` lists them. The stop guard's tier refusal stays: the hook is advice the model receives with its next request, and the guard is the catch for a reply that still omits the line. The observation left: the two hooks seen in `status/switches.txt` of a session's scratchpad at the owner's next switch.

Acceptance: a PreModelSwitch hook shows what a switch will cost (the tokens the next request re-sends, whether the cache is warm, the estimated cost of writing it) before it happens, and a PostModelSwitch hook gives the model its tier guidance after any switch, an automatic one included; the tier gate is retired for them, or the reason it cannot be is written here.

Address: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, read on 2 November 2026, with https://code.claude.com/docs/en/prompt-caching.

Gate whose job it does: the tier gate, in hook-dispatch.mjs and the tier line of stop-guard.mjs.

Depends: the harness version this container runs. The page says PostModelSwitch needs Claude Code 2.1.251 or later, and this container's version was not read.

### P-20. The Stop hook block cap

Repository: hub. Goal: 6. State: done. Plan: i-m-not-british-make-indexed-riddle.md.

The marker's log held nine calls, the first with the loop flag false and the other eight true. The hold's count is seven, the cap less one, and the measurement left it unchanged. The cases are the hold case and the plant case, with six plants (cap_hold_count, cap_hold_reset, cap_plant_armed, cap_plant_limit, cap_plant_log and cap_plant_line), all caught through the plants option, and the whole suite passed 261 of 261 when the guard was built. The guard itself refuses at most twice in one continuation chain, so the hold matters at the repeat refusal, where other hooks' refusals in the same chain could bring the count to the cap. The variable in the environment stays open under P-12. The record is plans/stop-cap-record.md.

Address: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks and https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars, read on 2 November 2026. The cap resets each time Claude calls a tool, and the hub's own stop hooks count against it.

## Siblings owing a hub pin or a sweep caller

The hub's pin check, run from the hub on the repository, exits 0: every pin names a commit the hub's main reaches, and the doctrine marker names a commit the hub has. And a branch-sweep workflow stands on the repository's default branch and calls the hub's sweep, so a dry run lists its session branches. Each is worked in that repository's own session. The hub's history was rewritten on 18 September 2026, so a pin made before it names a commit off the hub's main line (the instruction file, read 19 September 2026), and it is re-pinned in each repository separately.

## Findings

One entry for each key of the Found queue in the hub's notes, 127 in all, with the key in the title. The state is open unless the entry says it is answered by an item above. A queue line that records its own fix stays open until the plan that answers the queue reads the fix's case in a full run and drops the line. The first thirteen are the keys the audit of 1 November 2026 names, in the audit's order; the rest follow by key.

Repository: hub. Goal: 4. State: parked. Plan: none.

Parked: this waits on the owner raising a rewrite of the hub's history, which the doctrine says is offered only when the owner raises it. It unblocks the choice between keeping the commit and rewriting, and with it the pins and sweeps P-35 to P-43 that a rewrite would move.

Acceptance: the owner has chosen between keeping the commit and rewriting the hub's history to change it, and a read of the remote's log shows the choice applied. If the history is rewritten, every pin and sweep that names the old commits is moved in the same piece of work (lesson 334).

Depends: the owner's choice; P-35 to P-43 if the history is rewritten.

It stands on the app's staging as 2.76 in one commit whose subject opens New and is "New: one panel for making an infrared look: three channel swaps, Sky and Foliage keyed on your photograph, one Strength, and Save as a look", with the commit gates green, main unmoved and no walk run; the Gates and Deploy runs for it were queued when last read. The plan's findings for the hub are filed as P-422 to P-434 and its findings for the app stand in the app's Found queue as Q-JPS-256 to Q-JPS-265.

### P-448. The infrared app's colour panel left a rim between its two keyed boxes, found on the device pass of 9 November

Repository: Jefferson-Photography-Studio. Goal: 5. State: done. Plan: The infrared panel's rim put right: where both the Sky and the Foliage box are keyed on the photograph, the two boxes share the whole hue circle between their two centres with a blend as wide as a band's edge, so no pixel between the trees and the sky is left unmoved, in the CPU render and the shader alike, proved by planted checks and landed on staging as an increment of 2.76, with no walk before the promotion

Done: steps 368 and 369 of that plan handed back DONE. Step 368 read the gap on the code as it stood before any edit, the fraction of a whole move being 1 at hue 0, 0.9149 at hue 64 and 0.4344 at hue 90 against known figures of 1, under 0.95 and under 0.65; it then added the exported function `panelBoxWeights` to `src/pipeline.ts`, called by the band stage and by the tile's measure, wrote the same rule branch for branch into the shader of `src/gl.ts`, and raised the preview version from 103 to 104; every planted check of the model read as known, the fractions at hues 0, 64, 116 and 180 reading 1, an edit carrying both centres with no box moved rendering as one carrying none, and step 367's nine plants reading as known again. Step 369 added a dated paragraph to record 084, queued the app's findings as Q-JPS-266 to Q-JPS-271, ran the preview-version record run, the build, the module map and every commit gate, and landed one commit on the app's staging whose subject opens Fixed and is "Fixed: the Sky and Foliage boxes no longer leave an unmoved edge between the trees and the sky", main unmoved, as an increment of 2.76 with no walk run. The Gates and Deploy runs for it were not yet listed at the agent's one read. The shader's branch could not be planted in the container; it stands beside the CPU's in the report and the agreement walk at the promotion compares the two renders. The look at the same photograph is the owner's, on the device on staging, and the promotion waits on the owner's go.

Acceptance: where both the Sky and the Foliage box are keyed on the photograph the two share the whole hue circle between their two centres, in the CPU render and the shader alike, so no pixel between the trees and the sky is left unmoved; a box keyed by name acts as before; planted checks read the gap before the change and its absence after; record 084 says so; the commit stands on staging as an increment of 2.76 with every gate green and main unmoved, and no walk runs before the promotion.

Depends: P-416.

### P-460. The infrared app's separation instrument: closed, with its field reading finished by the routes that answer

Repository: Jefferson-Photography-Studio. Goal: 5. State: done. Plan: The separation instrument closed in the program and its field reading finished by the routes that answer: the account Adobe publishes of its masks and the colour equalizer page of darktable read through the web fetch tool, the four pages already on disk read whole, every source that stays unread named with each route tried, the whole written into the science file and record 050 as one Docs commit on staging, and the item entered as done with the findings of both runs filed in one hub landing

Done: steps 372 to 376 of the plan before this one and steps 379 to 381 of this plan handed back DONE, with step 372 and step 376 each refused in a send and put right in the next. The instrument itself landed on staging as an increment of 2.77 under the commit whose subject opens New: the test page's Separation check tells water, built things, sky and trees apart, by lightness, texture, position and joined selections; it is three new modules and a press on the test page, with every commit gate green and no walk run. The field reading then landed as one Docs commit on staging, "Docs: the field reading of 9 November gains the pages reached by further routes, and names the ones that stay unread", with main unmoved: Adobe's own article on its masking tools and darktable's colour equalizer source were read through the web fetch tool and are held as that tool's account of them, the segmenter's documentation and an infrared processing article were read whole from disk (the segmenter names no class that is sky, water, a tree, a building or a boat), and Adobe's help pages, Capture One's support pages, RawPedia's local adjustments page and Adobe's Lightroom release notes stay unread with every route tried named in the science file. Nothing is ranked and no control is proposed. The Gates and Deploy runs for the Docs commit were in progress at the one read. The look at the three marina raws on the test page's Separation check is the owner's, on the device on staging, and the promotion waits on the owner's go. This entry was written by step 382, and not from the Found queue.

Acceptance: the test page's Separation check runs on the device for the three marina raws and for the first again; its field reading is in the science file and record 050 with each source's state, and the sources that stay unread are named with the routes tried; the commits stand on staging with every gate green and main unmoved; the findings of the runs are filed.

Depends: none.

### P-484. The infrared app's separation measure read on the device, and its field reading made first-hand

Repository: Jefferson-Photography-Studio. Goal: 5. State: done. Plan: The tap labelling on the test page, through to staging: the hub's scope check read as the hub has judged it, the documents of 9 November landed, RawPedia's page and the field's ways of taking a sample read whole and joined, the decision record written first, then named samples placed on the photograph, structure cues beside the eight, selections fitted from samples and read on the next photograph, released on staging as 2.78 and closed in the program

Ejected: steps 384, 385, 387 and 391 handed back DONE; step 386 ended REFUSED in its third send at 21:14 on 9 November when the Drive guard refused the metadata read of the one file its exact-name search returned, so RawPedia's local adjustments page stays unread, with the routes that answered being the folder found by its title and its listing refused, then the file found by its name and its read refused; step 388 ejected at the scope check at 21:38 and is counted done by decision. What was read: Adobe's help page on masking in Lightroom and the Camera Raw team's article of 28 September 2021 (read in part, the print cut in three places), Capture One's pages on combining masks and on the luma range, each read whole from a printed copy brought into the session by hand, and darktable's colour equalizer page read whole from its source text; 442 figures of the Separation check's first four presses were compared with their reports and none differs, the fifth press's figures standing in no file; the readings and the figures were judged and joined into one account in six parts by step 391. What moved the item: the plan-scope check's lines-read rule counted the app's staging diff against main, 3,232,879 lines with the unpromoted run in it, so no verdict can honestly be recorded and no commit made (Q-HUB-437). The three documents, the science file with its three further passages, record 050 with its further addition and the notes with their Found queue sentences and four new lines Q-JPS-277 to Q-JPS-280, stand staged in the app's working copy on staging, uncommitted, with the two hooks modified and unstaged; the remote's staging and main are unmoved. The landing is carried to the next plan, which changes the scope check's count first and then takes the commit; the item is re-ranked ahead of the tap labelling, which depends on it. This entry was written by step 389 and not from the Found queue.

Done: step 400 of the plan of 10 November, second send, landed the three documents of 9 November with the fifth press's figures as checked (none differing, and the two presses on one photograph agreeing), the pages read on 10 November with what they change in the account, record 050's rectangle row put right and the new record with its bullet, as one Docs commit on staging; the scope check at the hub's main counted 1,408 lines for the following commit and not millions, which step 394 had read at the hub's main as counting only the commits the remote's staging lacks (P-486).

Acceptance: the figures of the Separation check's first presses on the device, on the three marina photographs, are checked against their reports and stand in the science file with what they are and their limits; the pages of the field returned to the session by hand are read whole from those copies, each labelled by how it was read, with what each says a selection is keyed on, how selections are joined and how the program samples an area of the picture; what they say stands in the science file and record 050 with nothing ranked; the Found queue's lines on unread pages are answered; it stands as a Docs commit on staging, every gate green, main unmoved and no walk run.

Depends: P-460; P-486 for its landing.

### P-485. The infrared app's test page labels regions by a sample placed on the picture, so any photograph can be measured for what tells its parts apart

Repository: Jefferson-Photography-Studio. Goal: 5. State: done. Plan: The tap labelling on the test page, through to staging: the hub's scope check read as the hub has judged it, the documents of 9 November landed, RawPedia's page and the field's ways of taking a sample read whole and joined, the decision record written first, then named samples placed on the photograph, structure cues beside the eight, selections fitted from samples and read on the next photograph, released on staging as 2.78 and closed in the program

Acceptance: on the test page's Separation check a region of the photograph is marked by a sample placed on the picture, wider than a few pixels and of a size that is set, moved over the area and confirmed, with a magnified view above the finger on a touch screen, in the form the field's convention gives, read from its pages first; each sample is named for what it holds, water, built things, sky, foliage or a name given, a kind nobody marks is absent, and several samples can share a name; the check then counts every cue and pair of cues between each two names, fits selections, reads them on the next photograph and says when a sample holds too few pixels to measure; the decision record is written before the build; nothing of the editor changes.

Done: steps 392 to 406 of the plan of 10 November: RawPedia's local adjustments page, the field's pages on taking a sample and on structure measures were fetched and read to their end where they answered, and the pages that answered a challenge or not found are written down as unread; a second joined account was written; record 109 was written before the build; the samples, structure and surface modules, the cue module's names and exclusions and the test page's wiring were built to one interface, each module proved by planted checks in the container; and 2.78 stands on the remote's staging as one commit opening New, with the twenty-four checks green on the build, the scope judged IN-SCOPE over 1,723 lines read and main unmoved. What only a press on the device shows is the placing, the drag, the keys, the magnified view, the announcements and the time of the three fits at a count; no walk was run. The Gates and Deploy conclusions for the release commit were unread when the step handed back. Six findings about the app are filed as Q-JPS-289 to Q-JPS-294 in the app's notes.

Depends: P-484.

### P-557. The infrared app's test page makes a mask from samples and tries it on another photograph, with the decision record for the editor's mask written first

Repository: Jefferson-Photography-Studio. Goal: 5. State: done. Plan: A mask made from samples, toward the editor: the decision record written first, the field's ways of making a mask from marked samples and of copying one read whole, the device's seven presses read in place, the editor's mask code surveyed, then the mask itself on the test page, soft and following edges, tried on another photograph with what it takes shown first, released on staging as 2.79 and closed in the program

Done: steps 409 to 420 of that plan. Eleven pages of the field on making a mask from marked samples and on refining one to a picture's edges were fetched by the plan's own fetcher and read to their end, and the pages on copying a mask between photographs were read where they answered (darktable's three and RawPedia's, from disk), with Adobe's masking page and Capture One's help written down as unread after a 403 and a script challenge. The editor's mask code was surveyed for the wiring: it holds six mask kinds where record 042 counts five, and no copy of a mask between open photographs. The test page's seven presses of 10 November at 2.78 were read in place as figures. The decision record numbered 110 was written before the build, with the editor's end result at the head of its Context, and its roadmap bullet placed. The documents (the science file's passage, record 109's device pass, the record, the bullet and six Found queue lines) landed as one Docs commit on staging, the scope judged over 397 lines. The mask module `src/samplemask.ts` was built with 36 planted checks read as known, and a full frame of 580,948 counted pixels was fitted, weighed and drawn in 281 ms in the container. The test page now draws every placed name's mask over the photograph in the three renderings the record lists, with the share of each name it takes, lists every mask kept from another photograph whether or not its name is placed, tries it with what it takes shown, and keeps it or redoes it by samples. 2.79 stands on the remote's staging as one commit opening New, with the twenty-four checks green on the build, the scope judged IN-SCOPE over 850 lines read and main unmoved. Nothing of the editor changed and no walk was run. What only a press on the device shows is the pictures of each rendering for each name, the time of the count's mask stages, and how the Try, Keep and list controls read on the tablet. The Gates and Deploy conclusions for the release commit were not yet read when the step handed back. Eleven findings about the app are filed as Q-JPS-295 to Q-JPS-305 in the app's notes; the editor's wiring is entered as P-558 and eight findings about the hub as P-559 to P-566.

Acceptance: the decision record for a mask made from samples stands, written before any build, with the end result at the head of its Context; the test page makes the mask from the samples placed on it, a weight for every counted pixel, drawn over the photograph in each rendering the record lists, one of them refined to edges; a mask kept from one photograph is tried on another and drawn there with its shares before it is kept, and is redone there by samples; the editor's mask code is surveyed for the wiring; nothing of the editor changes.

Depends: P-485.

Done: Quietkeep 3.28.2 on staging grows "Make a set to try things on" by an appended stream (four roles with last acts 1, 6, 19 and 47 days back, three stretches, a day of five things, a waiting-for and three captures), held by test/test-set.test.ts with a planted break per state and recorded in docs/decisions/test-set-2026-11.md; staging eef81a4, Spine 989 and Deploy 984 green for that head; production still 3.26.1.

Acceptance: a reader of the staging build can load, from where the app's other sample sets are offered, a generated set of invented work whose dates and act times are all relative to the moment of loading; folded, it shows the weeks ahead with days in the next seven and days out to thirty that hold something, one day holding more than three things, a thing drawn as a stretch, somebody waited on and things still to sort, and it gives the review of roles an order of at least four roles whose last acts lie at different distances back; a test holds each of those states, the two sets the app already has are unchanged unless the design record shows why one must move, and CI's runs for the landed commit conclude success.

Depends: none.

### P-589. Q-HUB-535: The check before a push waits for a hand-back call that an agent under a restored plan mode never makes

Repository: hub. Goal: 5. State: done. Plan: none.

Done: The check before a push counts a pass written as the first line of an ended agent's final message, so it no longer waits for a hand-back call; a case in the push guard's tests holds it.

Acceptance: An agent sent under a plan mode the harness restored at a resume, ending with words and no hand-back call, is not read by the check before a push as still running, or the refusal says plainly that such an agent is sent again after the plan is approved at the button; a case in the guard's tests holds it.

Depends: none.
